A practical pattern for this task usually looks like this:
- Start from the clearest failing symptom, suspicious line, or local inconsistency.
- Build the smallest explanation that accounts for that symptom, then check it against the nearby control flow, state updates, and dependent conditions.
- Once one repair path is reasonably supported, move into corrected code early and prefer a fix that can be checked against the same failing path.
- After the edit, rerun that path or the most relevant edge case.
- Then do one small coupled-region scan: a directly connected guard, index update, return path, or call site that could still break the same fix.
- If the recheck is still off, refine the same local region before opening a distant new theory.
- Once the failing path and its directly coupled region look consistent, finalize the code.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- staying in diagnosis mode for too long without testing the next plausible edit;
- moving from a local repair into a fresh branch of speculative causes when the current path has not been rechecked yet;
- turning a focused fix into a broad rewrite unless the surrounding code clearly forces it.

from typing import List

class Solution:
    def gridGame(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        result = float("inf")
        left, right = 0, sum(grid[0])

        for a, b in zip(grid[0], grid[1]):
            right -= a
            result = min(result, max(left, right))
            left += b

        return result

It can collect either:
- top suffix: stay on top row until some column j >= i+1, then down. But if it stays on top through columns 0..i, those are zero, then collects top i+1..j. It cannot collect top suffix because it went down before top suffix. Could it go down at k <= i-1, then later move up? No, only right/down, no up. So max is max(top suffix, bottom prefix). Yes.

Let's think if there is a possibility that the original code's algorithm is wrong due to robot2's optimal strategy not simply max(top suffix, bottom prefix)? Let's derive more thoroughly to be certain. The grid is 2 x n. Robot1 path has exactly one down move. Robot2 path also exactly one down move. Robot1 wants minimize robot2's maximum points. For a fixed robot1 path (down at column i), the cells available to robot2 are top row columns i+1..n-1 and bottom row columns 0..i-1. But robot2 starts at top-left and can only move right/down. It can collect either:
- top suffix: stay on top row until some column j >= i+1, then down. But if it stays on top through columns 0..i, those are zero, then collects top i+1..j. It cannot collect bottom prefix after going down at j >= i+1 because bottom j..n-1 are zero (robot1 visited bottom i..n-1, and j>=i+1, so bottom j..n-1 zero). It could collect bottom prefix before? To collect bottom prefix, it must go down at column k <= i-1. Then it travels bottom from k to n-1, but bottom i..n-1 zero, so collects bottom k..i-1. It cannot collect top suffix because it went down before top suffix. Could it go down at k <= i-1, then later move up? No, only right/down, no up. So max is max(top suffix, bottom prefix). Yes.

Wait, what about robot2 going down at column i? It would collect bottom i..n-1 zero and top 0..i zero, total 0. Not max unless all zero. Fine.

What about robot2 going down at column j between 0 and i-1, then bottom row includes bottom i..n-1 zero, but also bottom j..i-1. To maximize bottom prefix, choose j=0, sum bottom[0..i-1]. What about if j=0, it also collects bottom 0..i-1, yes. If j>0, less. Good.

What about robot2 going down at column j >= i+1, top suffix top i+1..j, choose j=n-1, sum top[i+1..n-1]. Good.

Thus formula.

Need maybe if robot1 path down at column i, robot1 collects bottom i..n-1, but if robot2 goes down at k <= i-1, it traverses bottom row and reaches bottom i (zero) then bottom i+1..n-1 zero. It cannot collect top suffix. Good.

Need maybe if robot1 path down at column i, robot2 could go down at column k <= i-1 and then later? No up. Fine.

At last iteration, right=0. left after previous iterations is bottom prefix before last. result min with max(bottom prefix before last, 0) = bottom prefix before last. This corresponds robot1 down at last column, remaining bottom prefix all except last? Wait if robot1 down at last column, it collects top all and bottom last. Remaining bottom 0..n-2. The code at last iteration left = sum bottom[:n-2]? Let's trace n=3: before i=2, left after i=1 = b0+b1 = bottom[:2], which is bottom columns 0 and 1. Yes bottom prefix before last = bottom[:n-1]. Good. At first iteration, right = sum top[1:], left=0. Down at col0, remaining top suffix. Good.

Let's think if there is a possibility that the original code's algorithm is wrong due to robot2's optimal strategy not simply max(top suffix, bottom prefix)? Let's derive more thoroughly to be certain. The grid is 2 x n. Robot1 path has exactly one down move. Robot2 path also exactly one down move. Robot1 wants minimize robot2's maximum points. For a fixed robot1 path (down at column i), the cells available to robot2 are top row columns i+1..n-1 and bottom row columns 0..i-1. But robot2 starts at top-left and can only move right/down. It can collect either:
- top suffix: stay on top row until some column j >= i+1, then down. But if it stays on top through columns 0..i, those are zero, then collects top i+1..j. It cannot collect bottom prefix after going down at j >= i+1 because bottom j..n-1 are zero (robot1 visited bottom i..n-1, and j>=i+1, so bottom j..n-1 zero). It could collect bottom prefix before? To collect bottom prefix, it must go down at column k <= i-1. Then it travels bottom from k to n-1, but bottom i..n-1 zero, so collects bottom k..i-1. It cannot collect top suffix because it went down before top suffix. Could it go down at k <= i-1, then later move up? No, only right/down, no up. So max is max(top suffix, bottom prefix). Yes.

Wait, what about robot2 going down at column i? It would collect bottom i..n-1 zero and top 0..i zero, total 0. Not max unless all zero. Fine.

What about robot2 going down at column j between 0 and i-1, then bottom row includes bottom i..n-1 zero, but also bottom j..i-1. To maximize bottom prefix, choose j=0, sum bottom[0..i-1]. What about if j=0, it also collects bottom 0..i-1, yes. If j>0, less. Good.

What about robot2 going down at column j >= i+1, top suffix top i+1..j, choose j=n-1, sum top[i+1..n-1]. Good.

Thus formula.

Need maybe if robot1 path down at column i, robot1 collects bottom i..n-1, but if robot2 goes down at k <= i-1, it traverses bottom row and reaches bottom i (zero) then bottom i+1..n-1 zero. It cannot collect top suffix. Good.

Need maybe if robot1 path down at column i, robot2 could go down at column k <= i-1 and then later? No up. Fine.

This corresponds robot1 down at last column, remaining bottom prefix all except last? Wait if robot1 down at last column, it collects top all and bottom last. Remaining bottom 0..n-2. The code at last iteration left = sum bottom[:n-2]? Let's trace n=3: before i=2, left after i=1 = b0+b1 = bottom[:2], which is bottom columns 0 and 1. Yes bottom prefix before last = bottom[:n-1]. Good. At first iteration, right = sum top[1:], left=0. Down at col0, remaining top suffix. Good.

Let's think if there is a possibility that the original code's algorithm is wrong due to robot2's optimal strategy not simply max(top suffix, bottom prefix)? Let's derive more thoroughly to be certain. The grid is 2 x n. Robot1 path has exactly one down move. Robot2 path also exactly one down move. Robot1 wants minimize robot2's maximum points. For a fixed robot1 path (down at column i), the cells available to robot2 are top row columns i+1..n-1 and bottom row columns 0..i-1. But robot2 starts at top-left and can only move right/down. It can collect either:
- top suffix: stay on top row until some column j >= i+1, then down. But if it stays on top through columns 0..i, those are zero, then collects top i+1..j. It cannot collect bottom prefix after going down at j >= i+1 because bottom j..n-1 are zero (robot1 visited bottom i..n-1, and j>=i+1, so bottom j..n-1 zero). It could collect bottom prefix before? To collect bottom prefix, it must go down at column k <= i-1. Then it travels bottom from k to n-1, but bottom i..n-1 zero, so collects bottom k..i-1. It cannot collect top suffix because it went down before top suffix. Could it go down at k <= i-1, then later move up? No, only right/down, no up. So max is max(top suffix, bottom prefix). Yes.

Wait, what about robot2 going down at column i? It would collect bottom i..n-1 zero and top 0..i zero, total 0. Not max unless all zero. Fine.

What about robot2 going down at column j between 0 and i-1, then bottom row includes bottom i..n-1 zero, but also bottom j..i-1. To maximize bottom prefix, choose j=0, sum bottom[0..i-1]. What about if j=0, it also collects bottom 0..i-1, yes. If j>0, less. Good.

What about robot2 going down at column j >= i+1, top suffix top i+1..j, choose j=n-1, sum top[i+1..n-1]. Good.

Thus formula.

Need maybe if robot1 path down at column i, robot1 collects bottom i..n-1, but if robot2 goes down at k <= i-1, it traverses bottom row and reaches bottom i (zero) then bottom i+1..n-1 zero. It cannot collect top suffix. Good.

Need maybe if robot1 path down at column i, robot2 could go down at column k <= i-1 and then later? No up. Fine.

This corresponds robot1 down at last column, remaining bottom prefix all except last? Wait if robot1 down at last column, it collects top all and bottom last. Remaining bottom 0..n-2. The code at last iteration left = sum bottom[:n-2]? Let's trace n=3: before i=2, left after i=1 = b0+b1 = bottom[:2], which is bottom columns 0 and 1. Yes bottom prefix before last = bottom[:n-1]. Good. At first iteration, right = sum top[1:], left=0. Down at col0, remaining top suffix. Good.

Let's think if there is a possibility that the original code's algorithm is wrong due to robot2's optimal strategy not simply max(top suffix, bottom prefix)? Let's derive more thoroughly to be certain. The grid is 2 x n. Robot1 path has exactly one down move. Robot2 path also exactly one down move. Robot1 wants minimize robot2's maximum points. For a fixed robot1 path (down at column i), the cells available to robot2 are top row columns i+1..n-1 and bottom row columns 0..i-1. But robot2 starts at top-left and can only move right/down. It can collect either:
- top suffix: stay on top row until some column j >= i+1, then down. But if it stays on top through columns 0..i, those are zero, then collects top i+1..j. It cannot collect bottom prefix after going down at j >= i+1 because bottom j..n-1 are zero (robot1 visited bottom i..n-1, and j>=i+1, so bottom j..n-1 zero). It could collect bottom prefix before? To collect bottom prefix, it must go down at column k <= i-1. Then it travels bottom from k to n-1, but bottom i..n-1 zero, so collects bottom k..i-1. It cannot collect top suffix because it went down before top suffix. Could it go down at k <= i-1, then later move up? No, only right/down, no up. So max is max(top suffix, bottom prefix). Yes.

Wait, what about robot2 going down at column i? It would collect bottom i..n-1 zero and top 0..i zero, total 0. Not max unless all zero. Fine.

What about robot2 going down at column j between 0 and i-1, then bottom row includes bottom i..n-1 zero, but also bottom j..i-1. To maximize bottom prefix, choose j=0, sum bottom[0..i-1]. What about if j=0, it also collects bottom 0..i-1, yes. If j>0, less. Good.

What about robot2 going down at column j >= i+1, top suffix top i+1..j, choose j=n-1, sum top[i+1..n-1]. Good.

Thus formula.

Need maybe if robot1 path down at column i, robot1 collects bottom i..n-1, but if robot2 goes down at k <= i-1, it traverses bottom row and reaches bottom i (zero) then bottom i+1..n-1 zero. It cannot collect top suffix. Good.

Need maybe if robot1 path down at column i, robot2 could go down at column k <= i-1 and then later? No up. Fine.

This corresponds robot1 down at last column, remaining bottom prefix all except last? Wait if robot1 down at last column, it collects top all and bottom last. Remaining bottom 0..n-2. The code at last iteration left = sum bottom[:n-2]? Let's trace n=3: before i=2, left after i=1 = b0+b1 = bottom[:2], which is bottom columns 0 and 1. Yes bottom prefix before last = bottom[:n-1]. Good. At first iteration, right = sum top[1:], left=0. Down at col0, remaining top suffix. Good.

Let's think if there is a possibility that the original code's algorithm is wrong due to robot2's optimal strategy not simply max(top suffix, bottom prefix)? Let's derive more thoroughly to be certain. The grid is 2 x n. Robot1 path has exactly one down move. Robot2 path also exactly one down move. Robot1 wants minimize robot2's maximum points. For a fixed robot1 path (down at column i), the cells available to robot2 are top row columns i+1..n-1 and bottom row columns 0..i-1. But robot2 starts at top-left and can only move right/down. It can collect either:
- top suffix: stay on top row until some column j >= i+1, then down. But if it stays on top through columns 0..i, those are zero, then collects top i+1..j. It cannot collect bottom prefix after going down at j >= i+1 because bottom j..n-1 are zero (robot1 visited bottom i..n-1, and j>=i+1, so bottom j..n-1 zero). It could collect bottom prefix before? To collect bottom prefix, it must go down at column k <= i-1. Then it travels bottom from k to n-1, but bottom i..n-1 zero, so collects bottom k..i-1. It cannot collect top suffix because it went down before top suffix. Could it go down at k <= i-1, then later move up? No, only right/down, no up. So max is max(top suffix, bottom prefix). Yes.

Wait, what about robot2 going down at column i? It would collect bottom i..n-1 zero and top 0..i zero, total 0. Not max unless all zero. Fine.

What about robot2 going down at column j between 0 and i-1, then bottom row includes bottom i..n-1 zero, but also bottom j..i-1. To maximize bottom prefix, choose j=0, sum bottom[0..i-1]. What about if j=0, it also collects bottom 0..i-1, yes. If j>0, less. Good.

What about robot2 going down at column j >= i+1, top suffix top i+1..j, choose j=n-1, sum top[i+1..n-1]. Good.

Thus formula.

Need maybe if robot1 path down at column i, robot1 collects bottom i..n-1, but if robot2 goes down at k <= i-1, it traverses bottom row and reaches bottom i (zero) then bottom i+1..n-1 zero. It cannot collect top suffix. Good.

Need maybe if robot1 path down at column i, robot2 could go down at column k <= i-1 and then later? No up. Fine.

This corresponds robot1 down at last column, remaining bottom prefix all except last? Wait if robot1 down at last column, it collects top all and bottom last. Remaining bottom 0..n-2. The code at last iteration left = sum bottom[:n-2]? Let's trace n=3: before i=2, left after i=1 = b0+b1 = bottom[:2], which is bottom columns 0 and 1. Yes bottom prefix before last = bottom[:n-1]. Good. At first iteration, right = sum top[1:], left=0. Down at col0, remaining top suffix. Good.

Let's think if there is a possibility that the original code's algorithm is wrong due to robot2's optimal strategy not simply max(top suffix, bottom prefix)? Let's derive more thoroughly to be certain. The grid is 2 x n. Robot1 path has exactly one down move. Robot2 path also exactly one down move. Robot1 wants minimize robot2's maximum points. For a fixed robot1 path (down at column i), the cells available to robot2 are top row columns i+1..n-1 and bottom row columns 0..i-1. But robot2 starts at top-left and can only move right/down. It can collect either:
- top suffix: stay on top row until some column j >= i+1, then down. But if it stays on top through columns 0..i, those are zero, then collects top i+1..j. It cannot collect bottom prefix after going down at j >= i+1 because bottom j..n-1 are zero (robot1 visited bottom i..n-1, and j>=i+1, so bottom j..n-1 zero). It could collect bottom prefix before? To collect bottom prefix, it must go down at column k <= i-1. Then it travels bottom from k to n-1, but bottom i..n-1 zero, so collects bottom k..i-1. It cannot collect top suffix because it went down before top suffix. Could it go down at k <= i-1, then later move up? No, only right/down, no up. So max is max(top suffix, bottom prefix). Yes.

Wait, what about robot2 going down at column i? It would collect bottom i..n-1 zero and top 0..i zero, total 0. Not max unless all zero. Fine.

What about robot2 going down at column j between 0 and i-1, then bottom row includes bottom i..n-1 zero, but also bottom j..i-1. To maximize bottom prefix, choose j=0, sum bottom[0..i-1]. What about if j=0, it also collects bottom 0..i-1, yes. If j>0, less. Good.

What about robot2 going down at column j >= i+1, top suffix top i+1..j, choose j=n-1, sum top[i+1..n-1]. Good.

Thus formula.

Need maybe if robot1 path down at column i, robot1 collects bottom i..n-1, but if robot2 goes down at k <= i-1, it traverses bottom row and reaches bottom i (zero) then bottom i+1..n-1 zero. It cannot collect top suffix. Good.

Need maybe if robot1 path down at column i, robot2 could go down at column k <= i-1 and then later? No up. Fine.

This corresponds robot1 down at last column, remaining bottom prefix all except last? Wait if robot1 down at last column, it collects top all and bottom last. Remaining bottom 0..n-2. The code at last iteration left = sum bottom[:n-2]? Let's trace n=3: before i=2, left after i=1 = b0+b1 = bottom[:2], which is bottom columns 0 and 1. Yes bottom prefix before last = bottom[:n-1]. Good. At first iteration, right = sum top[1:], left=0. Down at col0, remaining top suffix. Good.

Let's think if there is a possibility that the original code's algorithm is wrong due to robot2's optimal strategy not simply max(top suffix, bottom prefix)? Let's derive more thoroughly to be certain. The grid is 2 x n. Robot1 path has exactly one down move. Robot2 path also exactly one down move. Robot1 wants minimize robot2's maximum points. For a fixed robot1 path (down at column i), the cells available to robot2 are top row columns i+1..n-1 and bottom row columns 0..i-1. But robot2 starts at top-left and can only move right/down. It can collect either:
- top suffix: stay on top row until some column j >= i+1, then down. But if it stays on top through columns 0..i, those are zero, then collects top i+1..j. It cannot collect bottom prefix after going down at j >= i+1 because bottom j..n-1 are zero (robot1 visited bottom i..n-1, and j>=i+1, so bottom j..n-1 zero). It could collect bottom prefix before? To collect bottom prefix, it must go down at column k <= i-1. Then it travels bottom from k to n-1, but bottom i..n-1 zero, so collects bottom k..i-1. It cannot collect top suffix because it went down before top suffix. Could it go down at k <= i-1, then later move up? No, only right/down, no up. So max is max(top suffix, bottom prefix). Yes.

Wait, what about robot2 going down at column i? It would collect bottom i..n-1 zero and top 0..i zero, total 0. Not max unless all zero. Fine.

What about robot2 going down at column j between 0 and i-1, then bottom row includes bottom i..n-1 zero, but also bottom j..i-1. To maximize bottom prefix, choose j=0, sum bottom[0..i-1]. What about if j=0, it also collects bottom 0..i-1, yes. If j>0, less. Good.

What about robot2 going down at column j >= i+1, top suffix top i+1..j, choose j=n-1, sum top[i+1..n-1]. Good.

Thus formula.

Need maybe if robot1 path down at column i, robot1 collects bottom i..n-1, but if robot2 goes down at k <= i-1, it traverses bottom row and reaches bottom i (zero) then bottom i+1..n-1 zero. It cannot collect top suffix. Good.

Need maybe if robot1 path down at column i, robot2 could go down at column k <= i-1 and then later? No up. Fine.

This corresponds robot1 down at last column, remaining bottom prefix all except last? Wait if robot1 down at last column, it collects top all and bottom last. Remaining bottom 0..n-2. The code at last iteration left = sum bottom[:n-2]? Let's trace n=3: before i=2, left after i=1 = b0+b1 = bottom[:2], which is bottom columns 0 and 1. Yes bottom prefix before last = bottom[:n-1]. Good. At first iteration, right = sum top[1:], left=0. Down at col0, remaining top suffix. Good.

Let's think if there is a possibility that the original code's algorithm is wrong due to robot2's optimal strategy not simply max(top suffix, bottom prefix)? Let's derive more thoroughly to be certain. The grid is 2 x n. Robot1 path has exactly one down move. Robot2 path also exactly one down move. Robot1 wants minimize robot2's maximum points. For a fixed robot1 path (down at column i), the cells available to robot2 are top row columns i+1..n-1 and bottom row columns 0..i-1. But robot2 starts at top-left and can only move right/down. It can collect either:
- top suffix: stay on top row until some column j >= i+1, then down. But if it stays on top through columns 0..i, those are zero, then collects top i+1..j. It cannot collect bottom prefix after going down at j >= i+1 because bottom j..n-1 are zero (robot1 visited bottom i..n-1, and j>=i+1, so bottom j..n-1 zero). It could collect bottom prefix before? To collect bottom prefix, it must go down at column k <= i-1. Then it travels bottom from k to n-1, but bottom i..n-1 zero, so collects bottom k..i-1. It cannot collect top suffix because it went down before top suffix. Could it go down at k <= i-1, then later move up? No, only right/down, no up. So max is max(top suffix, bottom prefix). Yes.

Wait, what about robot2 going down at column i? It would collect bottom i..n-1 zero and top 0..i zero, total 0. Not max unless all zero. Fine.

What about robot2 going down at column j between 0 and i-1, then bottom row includes bottom i..n-1 zero, but also bottom j..i-1. To maximize bottom prefix, choose j=0, sum bottom[0..i-1]. What about if j=0, it also collects bottom 0..i-1, yes. If j>0, less. Good.

What about robot2 going down at column j >= i+1, top suffix top i+1..j, choose j=n-1, sum top[i+1..n-1]. Good.

Thus formula.

Need maybe if robot1 path down at column i, robot1 collects bottom i..n-1, but if robot2 goes down at k <= i-1, it traverses bottom row and reaches bottom i (zero) then bottom i+1..n-1 zero. It cannot collect top suffix. Good.

Need maybe if robot1 path down at column i, robot2 could go down at column k <= i-1 and then later? No up. Fine.

This corresponds robot1 down at last column, remaining bottom prefix all except last? Wait if robot1 down at last column, it collects top all and bottom last. Remaining bottom 0..n-2. The code at last iteration left = sum bottom[:n-2]? Let's trace n=3: before i=2, left after i=1 = b0+b1 = bottom[:2], which is bottom columns 0 and 1. Yes bottom prefix before last = bottom[:n-1]. Good. At first iteration, right = sum top[1:], left=0. Down at col0, remaining top suffix. Good.

=== RAW ANSWER ===
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def gridGame(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        result = float("inf")
        left, right = 0, sum(grid[0])

        for a, b in zip(grid[0], grid[1]):
            right -= a
            result = min(result, max(left, right))
            left += b

        return result
